或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保

1 
台積電可能與馬斯克合作興建並 
營運「Terafab」評析

報告日期： 2025/09/19 研 究 員：羅  金 鳳 (Sunny Lo)  
新 聞 評 析 
新聞重點摘要： 
台積電可能與馬斯克合作興建並營運「 Terafab 」。

一. 我們認為成案可能性極低  
我們認為台積電僅基於商業友好關係與馬斯克進行商討，但成案
的可能性極低，主因台積電表明過其晶圓廠的建置與營運都以
100% 自主擁有為主 。 雖然目前在德國的德勒斯登的ESMC 為合資
(台積電持股 70% 、博世持股 10% 、英飛凌持股 10% 、恩智浦持
股 10%) ，及在日本的熊本廠 JASM 為合資 (台積電持股 86.5% 、
Sony 持股  6.0% 、DENSO 持股 5.5% 、Toyota 持股 2.0%) ，但大
都因 2021 ～2022 年 COVID-19 期間車用晶圓持續供不應求，廠
商為了避免因半導體產能供貨不足而影響出貨的情況發生，紛紛
以能確保半導體晶圓供充足為優先，且後續接續 AI 需求蓬勃發
展，晶圓需求緊俏程度更加嚴重，各國紛紛力邀台積電到該國設
廠，日本及德國為汽車製造大國，因此，台積電於日本及德國的
設廠大都以車用的成熟製程或特殊製程為主 (僅 Sony 以 CMOS 影
像感測為主 )，在當地政府的建廠補貼下，也開放該國企業持有部
份股權。

我們認為本次傳言台積電可能與馬斯克合作興建並營運
「 Terafab 」的技術層次將會拉高到最先進製程的水準，一旦
Terafab 開始營運後，將直接與台積電的先進製程產生衝突，因
此，我們認為成案的可能性極低。馬斯克建置的晶圓廠初期的想
法也是因為晶圓代工產能持續供不應求，馬斯克將建置自有的晶
圓廠滿足旗下 Tesla 車用晶片 、Optimus 機器人 、Dojo 訓練晶片、
AI 晶片、低軌衛星晶片，尤其因目前 AI 的競爭發展白熱化，晶片
的運算能力及設計複雜度都大幅提升，使用最先進的製程節點勢
在必行，因此，馬斯克先前也曾於 2026 年 4 月宣布將與 Intel 合
作 14A 製程，導入 Terafab 進行生產製造，由於 14A 將是 Intel
下一階段最先進的製程，顯見馬斯克對最先進的製程有強烈的需
求，其 Terafab 勢必以發展最先進製程為主，因此，若台積電與
馬斯克合作，勢必也要以其最先進的製程 2nm 為主，甚至下一世
代的 16A ，才能滿足馬斯克的期待，如此，台積電先進製程機密
可能有外洩風險，且若如馬斯克宣稱的 Terafab 初期產能 10 萬片

/月，目前將達 100 萬片 /月，屆時晶圓代工產能將有過剩問題，因
為台積電 2025 年約當 12 吋晶圓的出貨量約 1,500 萬片 ，Terafab
若達 100 萬片 /月(年產能 1,200 萬片 )，將是一個龐大的產能的䆁
出。

目前台積電 2nm+3nm+5nm 佔總營收比重約 66% ，由於 ASP 相
對較高，我們推估其出貨量僅佔總出貨量的 35% ，毛利率也高於
平均水準 (雖然 2nm 仍在進行良率調整，尚未達公司目標，但其於
4Q25 開始量產，僅僅花兩個季度就已經開始賺錢 )，在 AI 需求強
勁的帶動下，台積電也非常極積的擴充 3nm 的產能，甚至將 5nm
的設備改機成 3nm ，顯見得 5nm 以下為台積電最賺錢的製程節
點，一旦與馬斯克合作 5nm 以下的製程節點，製程機密可能外流
的風險將遠高於未來可能接單的利益。考量台積電持續為 AI 浪潮
下的主要受惠者，先進製程、先進封裝同步大幅優於競爭對手，
預估台積電 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 為 108.1/142.76 元，投資建
議為買進，目標價 3141 元(22X2027 年稅後 EPS) 。

## Document 058: 2330台積電 群益

Source PDF: all report pdf/2330台積電 群益.pdf
Report source: 未公開來源
Date: 2026-10-04
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

產業速報  
                                        2026 年 9 月 9 日

1 群益投顧 
ASML (ASML US) 與台積電 (2330 TT) 共同推動 High NA EUV 將自現行的 6 吋光罩，
轉移至更大的 12 吋 光 罩。 目標於 2031 年之前建立 12 吋 光 罩 試 產 線(pilot line) ， 並 於
2033 年之前實現微影系統全面就緒。台積電並表示自 2030 年 起 ， 將 在 先 進 製 程 量 產
中導入 ASML 的 High NA  EUV 。台積電與 ASML 共同發起 6 吋光罩轉移至 12 吋光
罩 的 規 格 轉 移 ， 其 戰 略 意 義 在 於 ， 製 訂 規 格 和 標 準 ， 確 保 台 積 電 技 術 領 先 地 位 。 因 此
對 台 積 電 的 投 資 評 等 Buy ，目標價 3,200 元。

研 究 員 ： 陳 俐 妍   
ly.c@capital.com.tw

ASML 與 台 積 電 共 同 推 動 High NA EUV 將 自 現 行 的 6 吋 光 罩 ， 轉 移 至 更 大
的 12 吋 光 罩 ：  
09/07/2026 ASML 與 台 積 電 於 BACUS 國 際 光 學 工 程 學 會(SPIE) 先 進 微 影 技
術 研 討 會 前 宣 布 發 起 產 業 合 作 ， 共 同 推 動High NA EUV 將 自 現 行 的 6 吋光
罩 ， 轉 移 至 更 大 的 12 吋 光 罩 ， 預 期 將 進 一 步 提 升 晶 圓 廠 的 生 產 力 、 降 低 晶
片 製 造 成 本 ， 並 消 除 需 要 2 張 6 吋 光 罩 在 High NA EUV 曝 光 時 進 行 拼 接 的
限 制，使 整 個 半 導 體 產 業 受 惠。此 轉 移 能 讓 先 進 晶 片 製 造 服 務 提 供 者 充 分 利
用 High NA EUV 的 優 勢 ， 更 提 升 未 來 世 代 先 進 晶 片 的 成 本 效 益 。 
目標於 2031 年 之 前 建 立 12 吋 光 罩 試 產 線(pilot line) ，並於 2033 年 之 前 實
現 微 影 系 統 全 面 就 緒 ， 以 支 援 先 進 製 程 量 產 。 台 積 電 並 表 示 自2030 年 起 ，
將 在 先 進 製 程 量 產 中 導 入 ASML 的 High NA 技 術 。  
12 吋光罩 使 High NA 效 率 提 升 ：  
現行 EUV 設備的 NA(Numerical Aperture ， NA) 為 0.33 ， High NA 則 提 高 到
0.55 。 NA 數 值 越 高 ， 解 析 度 也 越 高 、 曝 光 時 能 呈 現 的 細 節 愈 多 。ASML 資
料顯示 High NA 可 將 最 小 解 析 尺 寸 從 現 行 EUV 的約 13 奈 米 進 一 步 縮 小 到
8 奈 米 ， 也 就 是 能 在 晶 圓 上 印 出 更 細 微 的 電 路 圖 形 。 部 分 原 本 需 要2 次 甚 至
更多次曝光才能完成的圖形，也有機會改以單次曝光處理，降低製程複雜
度 、 缺 陷 風 險 與 生 產 周 期 。 
舉 例 來 說 ，0.33 NA EUV 機 台 ， 至 少 需 要 4 張 光 罩 、 分 多 次 曝 光 才 能 完 成
A14 、 A10 金 屬 層 圖 形 ；High NA EUV ， 只 要 1 張 光 罩 就 能 完 成 相 同 圖 形 。
但 解 析 度 提 高 ，High NA 的 光 學 設 計 讓 單 次 可 曝 光 範 圍 縮 小 到 原 本EUV 的
50% ， 製 作 大 顆 晶 片 時 可 能 必 須 分 成2 次 曝 光 ， 再 把 圖 形 拼 接 起 來 。 這 也 是
台積電與 ASML 此 次 推 動 12 吋 光 罩 的 原 因 ， 相 較 現 行 6 吋 光 罩 ， 放 大 光 罩
尺 寸 預 期 能 進 一 步 提 升 晶 圓 廠 生 產 力、降 低 晶 片 製 造 成 本，並 消 除 High NA
曝 光 時 的 拼 接 限 制 。 
台 積 電 預 計 2030 年 開 始 使 用 High NA  EUV ， 推估 High NA 將 會 在 A10 或
A10 以 下 的 先 進 製 程 採 用：  
台積電預計 2030 年 開 始 導入 High NA  EUV ，比 12 吋 光 罩 微 影 系 統 預 計 全
面就緒的 2033 年 更 早 。 這 代 表 台 積 電 初 期 導 入High NA 時 ， 仍 會 先 使 用 現
行 6 吋 光 罩 ， 之 後 再 隨 12 吋 光 罩 與 相 關 系 統 成 熟 逐 步 轉 換 。

### Page 2

產業速報  
                                        2026 年 9 月 9 日

2 群益投顧 
根 據 台 積 電 目 前 公 開 的 製 程 藍 圖 ，A13 與 A12 製 程 都 預 計 2029 年 量 產 。 其
中 A13 是 A14 平 台 的 延 伸 ，A12 則 將 超 級 電 軌(Super Power Rail) 背面供電
技術導入 A14 平 台 。 因 此 N2 、 A16 、 A14 、 A13 與 A12 都 早 於 台 積 電 預 計
2030 年 導 入 High NA ， 推 估 High NA 將 會 在 A10 或 A10 以 下 的 先 進 製 程 採
用。  
Intel (INTC US) 和 Samsung (005930 KS) 領 先 台 積 電 採 用 High NA EUV ，但
也 同 時 參 與 12 吋 光 罩 開 發 ：  
Intel 在 2024 年 宣 布 完 成 全 球 首 套 商 用 High NA EUV 設 備 的 組 裝 ；2026 年
High NA 已 經 正 式 進 入 量 產 ， 並 用 於 部 分 18A 製 程 層 。 是 目 前 High NA 導
入 進 度 最 快 的 晶 片 製 造 商 。Samsung 則預計 2028 年導入 DRAM 量 產 ； 台 積
電選擇在 2030 年 導 入 。Intel 和 Samsung 也 同 時 表 示 與 ASML 及 供 應 鏈 合
作 ， 推 動 未 來 轉 向 大 尺 寸 光 罩 。 
台 積 電 與 ASML 共 同 發 起 6 吋 光 罩 轉 移 至 12 吋 光 罩 的 規 格 轉 移，將 領 導 業
界 規 格 ：  
6 吋 光 罩 轉 移 至 12 吋光罩 的 這 項 升 級 非 單 純 的 尺 寸 放 大 ， 而 是 面 臨 生 態 系
與 物 理 極 限 的 多 重 挑 戰 。 整 體 供 應 鏈 重 構 ， 包 括 基 板 與 材 料 以 及 設 備 等 等 。
12 吋 光 罩 石 英 基 板 的 平 坦 度 、 缺 陷 密 度 與 熱 膨 脹 係 數 控 制 難 度 倍 增 。 製 程
設 備 如 薄 膜 沉 積、蝕 刻、清 洗 與 自 動 化 搬 運 設 備 皆 需 重 新 開 發。後 續 檢 測 與
量測極限檢測難度也增加，更大面積的圖形缺陷檢測（ Inspection ）與修補
技 術 需 要 突 破 。 在 12 吋 面 積 上 維 持 奈 米 級 的 對 位 精 度 與 線 寬 均 勻 性 極 為 困
難   
電 子 設 計 自 動 化 (EDA) 工 具 必 須 重 新 適 應 大 尺 寸 曝 光 視 場 與 資 料 處 理 。 12 
吋 光 罩 的 圖 形 檔 案 與 運 算 複 雜 度 將 大 幅 上 升 。 
台積電與 ASML 共 同 發 起 6 吋 光 罩 轉 移 至 12 吋 光 罩 的 規 格 轉 移，其 戰略意
義在於 ，台 積 電 為 全 球 最 大 晶 圓 代 工 廠，為 先 進 製 程 最 大 的 供 應 商，可 以 領
導 光 罩 基 底 供 應 商 、 光 罩 檢 測 設 備 商， 甚 至 EDA 廠 等 整 個 生 態 系 ，除 共同
分攤 ， 跨 越 升 級 12 吋 光 罩 的 龐 大 研 發 成 本外 ， 製 訂 規 格 和 標 準 ， 確 保 台 積
電 技 術 領 先 地 位 。因 此 對 台 積 電 的投 資 評 等 Buy ， 目 標 價 3,200 元。

產業速報  
                                        2026 年 9 月 9 日

3 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】 
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy)  5%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等 
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整 
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】 
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」及 「 區 間 操 作 」均有 upside 目 標 價 。差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。 
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。 
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ： 
1.  達 目 標 價 。 
2.  雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。 
3.  推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 。

【免責聲明】  
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」 。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

## Document 081: 260930_daiwa_delta

Source PDF: all report pdf/260930_daiwa_delta.pdf
Report source: 未公開來源
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We provide our views on Delta after c.4 months of share price 
weakness, driven by investor concerns on HVDC delays, and a slowdown 
in power consumption trend amid deceleration in Nvidia’s GPU shipment 
growth YoY. We remain positive on Delta over 2026-28E, as rising AI ASIC 
server trend should drive a meaningful increase in DC-DC power module 
ASPs (vs. Nvidia’s discrete form factor). We expect higher HVDC adoption 
from 2028E, driven by IT rack and power supply architecture changes.

What’s the impact: Component shortages are the key near-term 
bottleneck. We attribute the recent share-price weakness to slower-than-
expected revenue momentum in both 2Q26, due to vendor-sanction issue 
at Delta Thailand, and 3Q26E, due to a worsening shortage of power 
semiconductors. We believe the 2Q26 revenue shortfall was a one-off 
event, though 3Q26E growth is likely to remain constrained by the power-
semi shortage. While supply capacity should improve, the supply-demand 
gap will likely persist for some time. That said, end-demand should remain 
sound. Given the continued improvement in revenue momentum, we still 
expect operating margin to reach a record-high of 18.3% in 3Q26.

Long-term growth drivers. We see Delta as a key beneficiary of the AI 
ASIC server trend. We were previously concerned that rising AI ASIC 
adoption could hurt Delta, as ASICs have lower thermal design power 
(TDP) than Nvidia GPUs, but now believe that Delta will benefit from rising 
content value of DC-DC power modules, as hyperscalers are more willing 
to spend on DC-DC modules (vs. discrete) despite higher initial cost given 
their superior energy efficiency. We forecast DC-DC power module revenue 
to reach c.TWD60bn in 2026E (vs. TWD9bn in 2025 and our prior estimate 
of c.TWD20bn for 2026E). This figure could increase further with rising 
ASIC usage in 2027. We also believe HVDC adoption rate could reach at 
least 50% in 2028E (vs. our prior expectation of 30%), as power supply 
architecture is becoming increasingly bulky (to accommodate aluminium 
capacitors inside power supply amid the super capacitor shortage). Limited 
space in IT racks would force customers to adopt more power racks and 
likely equip them with high voltage versions to improve efficiency.

What we recommend: We raise our 2026-28E EPS by 2-15% on higher 
HVDC contribution. We lift our 12M TP to TWD3,088 (from TWD2,988) 
based on a lower target PER of 49x (from 55x) to reflect market sentiment, 
(at the high-end of its past-3-year range of 11-50x) applied to our 1-year-
forward EPS. Key downside risk: lower-than-expected demand.

How we differ: Our 2028E earnings is 21% above the Bloomberg 
consensus, likely due to our more bullish assumption for HVDC adoption.

30 September 2026

Company profile 
Founded in 1971, Delta Electronics has been the global leader in switching power supply solutions since 
2002 and DC brushless fans since 2006. Its business covers 3 key areas: power electronics (eg, power 
supplies, thermal management products and passive components), automation (eg, controllers, robot 
arms and building management systems), and infrastructure (eg, datacentre infrastructure, networking 
and EV charging).

### Page 5

5

Delta Electronics (2308 TT): 30 September 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 30 Sep 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 1
Delta's board has 7 non-independent and 5 independent directors with a term of three years.
The total number of directors and ratio of independent directors appear higher than most other
listed companies. Besides, according to Delta's CSR report, enhancing the board's functions is
one of the key goals for the company to achieve better corporate governance; also, the board
members' diversity and professionalism will be taken into consideration to ensure a good board
quality. Moreover, the company evaluates all the directors in the board using internal regulations
and an external independent institution each year and at least once every three years,
respectively. We believe Delta has implemented relatively more enhanced measure than most
listed firms to improve its board quality.
Capital management 2
During 2016-19, Delta's payout ratio was stable in the range of c.70%, but fell to 56% in 2020
and 53% in 2021. For 2020, it was mainly due to a sizeable non-cash re-valuation gain after the
acquisition of Delta Thailand (DET), while the company maintained more cash on hand to deal
with the uncertainties caused by COVID-19. However, the DPS growth from 2016 to 2021 was
limited, as Delta struggled with business diversification into new end-markets (eg, smartphone,
NEV). We believe that Delta's effort started to bear fruit in recent years after its NEV business
has gradually picked up. Overall, we see a stable dividend policy from Delta and expect its DPS
to continue to grow YoY in the future given that they declared a TWD11.6 DPS for 2025 which
equals a 50% payout ratio.
Related party &
transaction 1 Revenue from related parties stood at 12% of total 2025 revenue and purchases from related
parties were at 11%. We see limited risk from related party transactions.
S Supply chain
management 1
Delta focuses on: 1) localised management, 2) supplier certification and risk management, and
3) supplier sustainability engagement as key initiatives under the scope of supply chain
management. In 2019, 144 new suppliers joined Delta's supply chain system. All of them passed
environmental and social evaluations, signed the declaration of "non-use of conflict materials",
and complied with Responsible Business Alliance's (BRA) Code of Conduct. Also, these vendors
had to implement and abide by Delta's supply chain CSR policy and code of conduct. To lower
ESG risks, Delta carries out RBA audits and monitors high-risk critical suppliers each year. The
suppliers that fail these audits have to provide detailed improvement plans in response to
identified issues. Besides, Delta has surveyed a total of 263 suppliers with potential risk and 63
suppliers with high risk of the total 1,331 suppliers, based on its CSR policy and code of conduct.
If these suppliers are flagged as high-risk and no concrete improvements are made, Delta may
consider terminating cooperation and transactions with them to improve the company's
engagement on supplier sustainability. Overall, we think Delta has a solid plan to improve its
supply chain management with specific KPIs, including: 1) achieving supplier improvement rate
of 80% in 2025, 2) reducing GHG emissions rate of critical suppliers by 10% in 2025, and 3)
improving water saving rate of critical suppliers by 12% in 2025.
S Data security 2
Delta's Information Security team created Delta's Information Security Policy in November 2013
and started providing educational training to global employees in 2014. Delta gradually
introduced the Information Security Management System (ISMS) in 2016 and obtained the ISO
27001 international certification in 2018. According to ISO27001, Delta has to conduct at least
one internal audit on information security and appoint an independent institution to conduct audit
externally. According to Delta, no major deficiencies have been found in recent years.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

Industry View In-Line
In this report, we focus on China's manufacturing PMI, which we
see as a potential catalyst for AirTAC's and Hiwin's share prices.
China's manufacturing PMI edged up  to 50.1 in September from 49.8 in August:
• This came in consistent with both our estimate and consensus of 50.1.
• The production index was up 1.3ppt while the new order index was down

0.1ppt MoM.
• Our macro team believes the upturn was led mainly led by a quarter-end
production push (see their report).
Our view:
• We believe the modest pickup in the  manufacturing PMI should help
sentiment  toward relevant stocks with high exposure to the Chinese market.
Among the automation names, we prefer plays with company-specific drivers
like AirTAC and Hiwin:
° AirTAC (1590.TW, OW) should continue to benefit from its own share
gains and incremental contribution from linear guides. Valuation appears

undemanding at 17x 2027e P/E (vs. the 25x average since 2020).
° Hiwin (2049.TW, OW) should show more margin expansion in coming
quarters, supported by higher utilization and price hikes. We see
potential share price upside at the current 29x 2027e P/E vs. peak cycle
valuation of 35-40x.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
AirTAC International (1590.TW)
Base case, 29x 2027e P/E. We believe this methodology better captures earnings growth
momentum for industrial automation component plays (market sentiment moves along
with business cycles). As the industry enters an up-cycle, we think applying a near-peak cycle
valuation of 29x P/E is reasonable given the stronger business outlook.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger macroeconomic climate in China

n Stronger-than-expected demand from industrial automation
Risks to Downside
n Sharper economic downturn in China
n Miniature linear guideway business development taking more time
n Larger-than-expected investment in new products but weaker-than-expected demand
Hiwin Technologies Corp. (2049.TW)
Base case, P/E. We think this valuation methodology better captures Hiwin's earnings growth
momentum, as its valuation tends to move with the business cycle. We apply a target multiple

of 38x to our 2027 EPS estimate. We view this multiple as justified by the 47% operating profit
CAGR we project for 2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger industrial automation demand pickup.
n Market share gains.
n Earlier-than-expected revenue contribution from humanoids.
Risks to Downside
n Weaker industrial automation demand.
n More pricing pressure.
n Market share loss.
n More broad-based impact on the global economy from geopolitical risks.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

## Document 088: 261001_ml_nanyaPCB

### Page 1

NYPCB

Alarm cleared with new drivers into 2027; 
re-it. Buy with PO raise to NT$2,000

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 2,000 TWD | Price: 1,340 TWD

BT substrate price hike resumed from 4Q26 
Following (see report) the worries of moderating BT substrate price hike in early 
September, we now expect NYPCB to still raise relevant ASP by single-digit % in 4Q26, 
and the hike is purely driven by favorable S/D which will fully translate into the accretion 
in bottom-line. Following this, BT substrate price will likely be raised by another 20+% in 
1Q27, due partly to the cost pass-through. We currently bake in ~10% revenue QoQ with 
32-33% GM for 4Q26, but we do not rule out the possibility for its revenue to surprise 
on the upside, should there be a faster ramp of its ABF substrate capacity in Kunshan. 
New driver for 2027: server CPU & mix optimization  
Looking into 2027 we expect the benefits of demand overflow on (see report) server 
CPU side to materialize, and we flag a chance for the firm to record 20+% revenue from 
the emerging business with AMD server CPU, where NYPCB is working on the 
qualification in its Shulin site currently and should capture most of the spillover demand 
if the progress goes smooth. Meanwhile, the firm will likely allocate more capacity from 
other sites (e.g. Kunshan) to fulfill the demand from Broadcom, which also helps enhance 
the mix. Currently we model ~50% GM for its ABF substrate business in 2027, but we 
see chance for further upward revision, driven by more favorable S/D dynamics and mix.  
Raise estimates/PO; prime beneficiary of price hike 
NYPCB’s share price (up ~50%) lags its major peers Unimicron/Kinsus (up ~70%) since 
late-July, but in our view it is still the best proxy to position for price hike in the 
substrate market. Judging from (see report) Kinsus’ August prints last week, we believe 
NYPCB remains on track to meet or beat the market’s expectations on N-T margins, and 
we reiterate Buy on the stock. Along with 13%/18% EPS raise in 2027/28E thanks to the 
more benign pricing/margin outlook driven by further mix improvement and S/D, we 
raise PO to NT$2,000 (from NT$1,700) same based on 35x 2H27-1H28E P/E.

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
Founded in 1997, NYPCB was listed on the Taiwan Stock 
Exchange in 2006. The firm is a 67%-owned subsidiary of 
Nan Ya Plastics Corporation (1303 TT). NYPCB's main 
products are HDI, PCB and IC substrates (including ABF and 
BT substrates), widely used in telecommunication, 
consumers, PC, and Auto applications. The clients include 
AMD, Broadcom, Samsung, Apple, Continental, etc. The 
main competitors include Unimicron, Kinsus, Ibiden, 
Shinko, and SEMCO, etc.

Investment Rationale 
We have a Buy rating on NYPCB, in view of its continuous 
improvement of utilization rate and gross margin across 
ABF and BT substrate business into 2027, thanks to the 
robust demand growth from fabless customers and 
demand overflow in AI ASICs.

Price objective basis & risk 
NYPCB (NANYF) 
Our PO of NT$2,000 is based on 35x 2H27-1H28E P/E. We view this multiple as fair 
given ROE and operating margin expansion to 39% and 41% in 2027-28E (vs 13% and 
14% in 2025-26E).

Upside risks to our PO are (1) tighter ABF supply, (2) higher SiP/AiP adoption, and (3) 
less intensified competition pressure in PCB business.

Downside risks are (1) greater ABF expansion from peers, (2) muted SiP/AiP adoption, 
and (3) more severe competition in PCB business.

of potential price fluctuation, are: A - Low, B - Medium and C - High. INVESTMENT RATINGS reflect the analyst’s assessment of both a stock’s absolute total return 
potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment rating s are: 1 - Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment 
rating of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things, 
the 0-12 month total return expectation for a stock and the firm’s guidelines for ratings dispersions (shown in the table below). The current price objective for a 
stock should be referenced to better understand the total return expectation at any given time. The price objective reflects the analyst’s view of the potential price

## Document 091: 261005_daiwa_GUC

Source PDF: all report pdf/261005_daiwa_GUC.pdf
Report source: 未公開來源
Date: 2026-10-05
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Summary: Global Unichip (GUC) reported September revenue which closed its 3Q26 top-
line 19% above market expectations thanks to its stronger-than-expected turnkey revenue 
run-rate during the quarter, likely attributable to more capacity support from its key foundry 
helping GUC meet strong demand from customers for AI projects. We see upside risk to 
our/consensus 3Q26 bottom-line estimates but are currently comfortable with our 4Q26 
forecasts despite the 3Q26 revenue beat likely dwarfing QoQ growth for 4Q26. We have a 
Buy (1) rating on the counter and will review our model in due course post its quarterly 
results release.

Highlight 
• 3Q26 top-line beating expectations. GUC released September revenue of TWD7.1bn, 
up 22% MoM (+98% YoY, see chart below), which closed its 3Q26 top-line at 
TWD18.8bn, up 35% QoQ (+118% YoY) for a new record. 3Q26 revenue beat our and 
consensus estimates both by 19% each thanks to its turnkey business (ie, mass-
production) which contributed TWD16.7bn for 89% of total and beat our estimate by 
22%. We attribute the beat to likely its key foundry continuously unleashing extra 
capacity helping GUC meet the strong customer AI demand during the quarter.

• Quarterly results preview and outlook. We expect GUC to have earned 3Q26 net 
profit of TWD1.7bn, varying not much from the consensus estimate, yet given the top-
line beat, we now see upside risk to the market expectations. As for 4Q26, our top-line 
and bottom-line forecasts are currently 10% and 26% above the consensus, 
respectively, making us comfortable with our current forecasts since we see the 3Q26 
beat being driven by increased foundry capacity allocation for some shipment pull-in, 
thus dwarfing 4Q26’s QoQ growth on intact AI demand. As flagged in our previous note 
(Upgrading: unleashed capacity adding to growth, 5 August 2026), we continue to 
expect GUC to be a key beneficiary from the strong AI ASIC ramps, including AI CPU 
and accelerators.

Recommendation 
We rate GUC Buy (1) with a 12M TP of TWD 5,125 on our 4-quarter forward PER target of 
63x. The stock performed well recently which we see as largely pricing in the 3Q26 top-line 
beat, therefore only suggesting investors accumulate only on any technical pullback. Key 
downside risks are less foundry support and margin execution.

UMC (2303.TW) From T o

TSMC (2330.TW) From T o
Price Target NT$2,988.00 NT$3,088.00
We think the capex budget might gradually shift to AI compute
and AI networking in 2028, as memory usage needs to be
optimized. We see positives for TSMC and UMC.
TSMC 3Q26 earnings preview: We expect the company to raise full-year revenue
guidance to the low-to-mid-40% range (vs.  above 40%) Y/Y, implying 4Q26 revenue

will rise 10%-15% Q/Q amid very strong 3nm demand. 3Q26 gross margin should
reach the high end of guidance of 67.5% However, should Intel focus on internal
capacity expansion, we think TSMC would maintain 2nm capacity expansion at the
same pace and its capex at US$54bn for 2026. For 2027, we see TSMC’s capex
reaching US$75bn to US$80bn.
In terms of 2027 wafer price hikes, we see leading edge (7nm and below) up 5%-10%
and mature nodes up 0%-3%; hence, gross margin could approach 70% in 2027. As a

result, we raise  our TSMC price target  by 3% to NT$3,088.
Key topics for upcoming TSMC 3Q26 earnings call on October 15:
1. Would TSMC consider working with TereFab  (Taiwan News, Oct 3 2026) and
building new fab sites in Texas (Bloomberg, Oct 1 2026)? Our view is that expansion
in Texas could be necessary, considering customer demand and limited space at the
Arizona fab in five years.
2. If Intel were to increase capex, would that reduce outsourcing at TSMC? Our

recent industry checks suggest some trimming of Intel’s 3nm and 2nm; how might
TSMC reallocate its resources?
3. How would TSMC address large reticle size chip demand? Our checks suggest
MediaTek handled TPU v10 IceFish reticle size initially at 14x; could TSMC’s CoWoS
or CoPoS be able to support such packaging?
We raise  our UMC price target by 36% to NT$188 on AI networking (SiPh)
business upside: Some investors remain on the sidelines amid ongoing smartphone

and PC demand weakness. We have seen some order cuts in PC semis recently, as
some consumer analog IC customers (e.g., Silergy) have not been able to pass
through UMC’s wafer price hike. Therefore, some investors do not expect any UMC’s
price hike in 2027. However, two growth drivers could help UMC raise wafer price
for 8-inch in 2027: 1) Samsung DDI outsourcing, as we noted in our earlier  Foundry
Floorplan, and 2) growing SiPh PIC demand, in particular after UMC introduced its

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

TSMC 2nm and 3nm capacity growth remains robust; 5nm
set to decline
Consistent with our demand forecast, TSMC indicated very strong capacity requirements
for both 2nm and 3nm starting in 2026, implying a 70% capacity CAGR for N2 from 2026

to 2028. TSMC also suggested that 5nm capacity will begin to decline from 2027, in line
with our forecast that 5nm demand will drop as Blackwell transitions to Rubin on 3nm.
Separately, advanced packaging capacity expansion should remain in focus for TSMC in
2027. This includes, but is not limited to, CoWoS and SoIC, for which we now expect
capacity to reach 200kwpm and 40kwpm, respectively, by 2027.

How might TSMC address Intel's competition in both wafer
foundry and advanced packaging?
Intel EMIB-T remains competitive in 2029; how TSMC will
address?
With demand in AI continuing to boom, we expect TSMC and its OSAT partners to
expedite capacity expansion plans for CoWoS. We see TSMC's CoWoS capacity building
further to 260kwpm in 2028, from 130/200kwpm in 2026/2027, with 2028's expansion
mainly focused in Arizona's AP9/10 and potentially in AP7. TSMC is now conducting trial

production for CoPoS in Taoyuan. If the progress goes well, we would expect TSMC to
start building its CoPoS capacity, with location also likely to be in AP9/10 or AP7P3.
We expect the non-TSMC camp to expand CoWoS capacity to 110kwpm by 2028 year-end,
from 80kwpm by end-2027. For ASE/SPIL, we expect FoCoS+CoWoS capacity to rise from
30kwpm at 2027-end to 50-60kwpm at 2028-end, with expansion focused on CoWoS-L
and CoWoS-R. For Amkor, we expect CoWoS capacity to increase from 30kwpm at 2027-

end to 55-60kwpm at 2028-end, also focused on CoWoS-L and CoWoS-R.

What about  Intel EMIB capacity build?
For Intel, we see current average EMIB-M capacity of 110kwpm falling slightly to 95kwpm
in 2027 as it converts some toward EMIB-T. We see the major customers of EMIB-M being
Trainium3, upcoming Trainium 4 and its in-house server CPU. Average EMIB-T capacity is

around 5kwpm in 2026, which we expect to further scale to 15-20kwpm in 2027 and 40-
45kwpm in 2028. All of the capacity mentioned above is converted for  12-inch wafer
equivalent, which we believe would be much more easier to communicate with compared
to TSMC CoWoS capacity.
Although some small projects are ongoing, we still see MediaTek/Google Humufish
consuming most of the capacity during 2027-28. We maintain our view that Intel EMIB-T

Indeed, we have seen some TSMC customers surveying (testing vehicles) Intel EMIB-T due
to the design for larger chip size, lower cost, and intention to seek a second source
because TSMC's CoWoS capacity is still relatively tight. However, we believe TSMC could
still try to expand its packaging size by leveraging different technologies to meet
customers' needs. We think it remains concerned about whether it is going to open its
front-end computing die production/know-how to its competitor, Intel, for back-end

packaging.
Our prediction of the trend after 2028 –  TSMC might:
1. Pull in CoPoS (panel-level process) with both 300mm and 500mm square panel
size
2. Introduce 3.5D packaging with SoIC for vertical chip stacking (Meta MTIA is
surveying)
3. Adopt glass core substrate to solve warpage for >9.7x reticle-size chips

Pros and cons of TSMC's CoWoS, Intel's EMIB, TSMC's
CoPoS, and SoIC
TSMC’s CoWoS, Intel’s EMIB, TSMC’s CoPoS, and TSMC’s SoIC represent different points
along the advanced packaging spectrum, each optimizing for a different balance of
bandwidth, cost, scalability and integration complexity. We think future design of the
chips is likely to combine these approaches—for example, using CoWoS for lateral

integration and SoIC for vertical stacking—to achieve optimal system-level performance.

• CoWoS excels in high-bandwidth 2.5D integration but is costly and capacity-limited
• EMIB offers a more cost-effective and modular alternative with some performance
trade-offs
• CoPoS targets large-scale, cost-efficient integration but is still maturing
• SoIC delivers the highest performance through 3D stacking at the expense of
complexity and thermal constraints.

10
UMC is well positioned for Silicon Photonic IC (PIC) foundry
In our previous Foundry Floorplan, we presented a constructive view on UMC given its AI-
related revenue. Management indicated AI revenue of US$300mn this year (3-5% of
revenue), potentially more than tripling to US$1bn in three years (2029).
UMC has a  capacity expansion plan to capture AI demand, including a clean room for
advanced packaging for AI chips, such as wafer-on-wafer and memory-on-memory stacking.

Some investors remain on the sidelines amid ongoing smartphone and PC demand
weakness, and we have seen some order cuts of PC semis recently. Some think a UMC
price hike in 2027 wouldn’t go through, but we see two growth drivers helping it
successfully  hike wafer price for 8-inch in 2027: 1) Samsung DDI outsourcing, and 2)
growing SiPh PIC demand, in particular after UMC introduced its 400Gb per lane solution
(PIC + TFLN) solution.
How UMC is differentiated in PIC foundry

According to UMC, InP and TFLN are the two most promising solutions, as they are much
more mature than the other contenders. InP is facing severe shortage, adding to TFLN’s
momentum.
UMC Group’s TFLN program started in 2021 with 6” in mass production and 8” in
sampling. TFLN's   strengths include low loss, high bandwidth (> 110 GHz), good thermal
stability, and strong linearity.
Integrating TFLN with PIC is a challenge as well as an opportunity. Advanced packaging

schemes and micro transfer printing are both seen as promising candidates. UMC is
developing the integration technology, leveraging its expertise in PIC, TFLN, and advanced
packaging.
Adoption of higher-performance modulators (TFLN/InP) and improvement in photo diodes
are key factors affecting deployment of plain-vanilla (non-WDM, IM/DD) DCIs with 400
Gbps/channel performance and beyond, which is very close to TSMC's mainstream PIC
technology Micro Ring.

Exhibit 15: UMC differentiates in TFLN process offering for PIC
Source: UMC

About Foundry Floorplan
In 2011,  we first published this product (Asia/Pacific Foundry: The Foundry Floorplan) to
provide our analysis of foundry industry dynamics. We have resumed this practice as
global foundry competition remains active. Foundry supply is also a critical resource for
future AI chips, whether in the global market or China's local market.
Below are prior reports from this series:
• The Foundry Floorplan: All boats riding on the same tide – mature node up-cycle

begins (19 May 2026)
• The Foundry Floorplan: TSMC preview update; CoWoS vs. EMIB competition (14
Apr 2026)
• The Foundry Floorplan: Raise TSMC's 2027e and 2028e Capex (9 Mar 2026)
• The Foundry Floorplan: Update on TSMC's 2027 capex; interposer outsourcing to
Vanguard? (13 Jan 2026)
• Asia/Pacific Foundry: The Foundry Floorplan, April 2011

TSMC: Estimate and price target revisions
Our earnings forecasts increase 4% for 2026,  5% for  2027 and 3% for 2028: We raise
our 2026 revenue forecast and modestly increase our margin assumptions to reflect
stronger-than-expected 3Q26 performance, driven by robust AI demand, including
sustained Blackwell shipments. We raise our 2027–28 revenue assumptions by 3%–5%,
reflecting the increase in strong leading-edge demand as well as the wafer price hike.

Our PT increases to NT$3,088 from   NT$2,988, driven primarily by our estimate

revisions. Our PT implies a 20x P/E multiple on our new 2027 EPS forecast. TSMC
currently trades at 17x that estimate, close to its average NTM P/E of 16.5x since 2018. We
find the stock attractive at current levels.
We continue to derive our 12-month price target (base-case scenario) using a residual
income valuation framework. Our key assumptions are unchanged, including a cost of
equity of 9.2% (beta of 1.2, equity risk premium of 6.0%, and risk-free rate of 2.0%), an

intermediate growth rate of 10.5%, and a terminal growth rate of 4%.
Our bull and bear case scenario values increase to NT$3,720 (from NT$3,600) and NT
$1,700 (from NT$1,645), respectively.

UMC: Estimate and price target revisions
We raise our 2026, 2027, and 2028 earnings estimates by 56%, 25%, and 31%,
respectively: Our 2026e change is due mostly to strong 2Q26 non-op gains. We increase
2027/28e revenue and EPS due to new CPO related business contribution from 12-inch
SiPh PIC  as well as 8-inch TFLN. Our 2028e increase also includes DDI outsource from
Samsung foundry.

Risk Reward - UMC (2303.TW)
NT$188.00
Base case, residual income model. Key assumptions: 9.2% cost of equity, 13.5% medium-term
growth, and 3.0% terminal growth.
Morgan Stanley EstimatesMean
Consensus Price Target Distribution NT$38.30 NT$258.00
Source: Reﬁnitiv, Morgan Stanley Research
MS Rating
▪ We are OW as we see structural changes
in the mature node industry's
supply/demand dynamics.
▪ Its foundry quality is comparable to
TSMC's in mature nodes. Along with

specialty technologies, this could help offset
part of the margin downside.
▪ Non-AI market recovery remains uncertain
in 2027, yet UMC should still beneﬁt from
strong AI-related demand spillover from its
foundry peers.
▪ 12nm FinFET development is strategic. It
should be one of UMC's long-term
strengths, notably when Chinese foundries
cannot effectively expand FinFET capacity.
▪ The stock now trades at 4.1x 2027e BVPS.
That is well above its historical average

since 2009, but our price target implies 5.1x.

64% Overweight

12% Underweight
Source: Reﬁnitiv, Morgan Stanley Research

View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here
NT$270.00
7.5x 2027e BVPS
Stabilized semi demand; better-than-
expected 8-inch and trailing-edge 12-inch
demand: 1) Global semi sales grow >15% Y/Y

in 2026, and ex-TSMC foundry industry
grows >10% Y/Y; 2) UMC gains share in both
8- and 12-inch wafers thanks to technology
differentiation; 3) UMC receives more
outsourcing from customers, which helps ﬁll
22/28nm capacity and lifts blended wafer
ASP; 4) traction increases for UMC's
12nm/14nm FinFET technologies.
NT$188.00
5.2x 2027e BVPS
Healthy semi growth; improving demand for
8-inch and trailing-edge 12-inch: 1) Global
semi sales grow ~15% in 2026 and ex-TSMC

foundry industry grows 3-5% Y/Y; 2) 8-inch
utilization rate is above 75% with steady 12-
inch demand amid semi inventory digestion;
3) UMC hikes wafer prices slightly in
response to strong AI-related demand.
NT$95.00
2.6x 2027e BVPS
Signiﬁcant semi industry decline; 8-inch and
trailing-edge 12-inch face weakening
demand: 1) Global semi sales grow only <5%
in 2026; 2) display semi and power semi
demand deteriorates further; 3) signiﬁcant
wafer pricing erosion given low fab

utilization and mature node peers'
competition; 4) depreciation ramp-up of
more than 30% Y/Y in 2026 without
sufﬁcient wafer order inﬂow to sustain
UMC's gross margin.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Chroma ATE (2360.TW)
06 October 2026 Citi Research
3
Chroma ATE
Valuation
Our target price of NT$2,500 is based on 40x 2027E P/E, at close to 1x PEG. Chroma shares have re-rated above its historical
range as its growth outlook is now strongly tied to AI infrastructure build out. We estimate 60%+ of its revenue is now AI-
related, with strong growth across product segments including power testers, SLT, metrology, and photonics. Chroma has a
commanding market share in most of these segments and should be well positioned to capitalize on the global AI expansion.

Risks
Downside risks to achieving our target price include: 1) softer AI/CPO growth expectation due to changes in capex outlook or
production bottleneck; 2) fewer SLT needed as a result of shortened cycle time as GPU yield rate improves; 3) disappointing
metrology shipment due to changes at ramp-up schedule; 4) testing process change leading to loss in market share; 5) FX risks
(50-60% of Chroma’s sales are in US$, 10-20% in Rmb).
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

## Document 106: 261006_ml_kingslide

Source PDF: all report pdf/261006_ml_kingslide.pdf
Report source: 未公開來源
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

King Slide

Maintain Buy: Sep sales -31% MoM from 
high bases; Aug earnings remain solid

Maintain Rating: BUY | PO: 14,888 TWD | Price: 13,100 TWD

Sep sales -31% MoM from high bases; 3Q26 sales in-line 
King Slide’s Sep sales of NT$4.46bn declined 31% MoM (+196% YoY), after the strong 
sales in the past few months (up 17-46% MoM in Apr-Jul and up 1% MoM in Aug). 3Q26 
sales of NT$17.34bn (+60% QoQ, +297% YoY) were 0.5% above BofAe of NT$17.26bn 
and 0.9% below consensus of NT$17.49bn.  
We are not surprised with the MoM sales drop in Sep as (1) the company’s MoM sales 
trend has been volatile (Exhibit 1 and Exhibit 2), (2) sales bases in Apr-Aug were at 
record levels, (3) contribution from Nvidia Rubin GPU projects should be more 
meaningful in 4Q26 after hardware components ramp-up, and (4) partial general server 
orders/demand likely were pushed out due to key component shortage (e.g. memory, 
CPU). 
Solid Aug EPS NT$40.07; pre-tax/net margin 74.6%/59.1% 
In addition to Sep sales, on the same day (6 Oct), King Slide also announced its Aug 
unaudited key financial data as required by local regulations (Exhibit 3). Aug sales were 
NT$6,466mn (+344% YoY), while pre-tax profit was NT$4,826mn (+216% YoY) and net 
income was NT$3,819mn (+212% YoY). EPS came in at NT$40.07 (+212% YoY). Pre-tax 
margin and net margin were 74.6% and 59.1%, respectively.  
Compared to July unaudited figures announced on 14 Aug (see our 17 Aug report), 
including sales of NT$6,407mn, pre-tax margin of 93.4%, net margin of 74.7%, and EPS 
of NT$50.24, we believe the margin and EPS differences mainly reflected FX impact 
under similar sales scale. 
Combining Jul and Aug unaudited results, sales totaled NT$12.87bn and EPS reached 
NT$90.31. Pre-tax margin and net margin were 84.0% and 66.9%, respectively. Jul + Aug 
sales achieved 75%/74% of BofAe/consensus 3Q26 sales. Pre-tax profit, net income, 
and EPS all achieved 79% of BofAe (79.4% pre-tax margin, 62.9% net margin, and 
NT$113.87 EPS) and 77-78% of consensus (80.3% pre-tax margin, 63.1% net margin, 
and NT$117.62 EPS). We also noted that Jul + Aug EPS of NT$90.31 already surpassed 
EPS of NT$74.38 in 2Q26 with similar pre-tax/net margin (84.0%/66.9% in Jul + Aug, vs. 
84.2%/65.4% in 2Q26). 
Maintain Buy; expect a neutral-to-positive market reaction 
We expect a neutral-to-positive market reaction in the near term in view of significant 
MoM sales decline in Sep but also still solid Aug unaudited data. We maintain Buy on 
King Slide as the server rail market leader with broad and diversified client/project base. 
We expect the company to post 3Q26 results after market close on 12 Nov (Thu).

Price objective basis & risk 
King Slide (KGSLF) 
Our PO of NT$14,888 is based on 32x 2027E EPS. We use 32x P/E multiple (2.5SD 
above P/E average since listing) in view of strong AI server market demand, leading 
position in server rail, solid/high margin profiles, and double-digit sales/EPS CAGR in 
2026-28E. Its early step into server market since 2001 with proven track record, much 
larger scale than peer, and 3,600+ patents as strong moats for high/above-peer margins. 
Its above-30x P/E is a similar case to other component sub-sector leaders in Taiwan.

Upside risks to our PO are: (1) even stronger AI server demand, (2) margin upside thanks 
to larger scale and/or better efficiency, (3) better kitchen slides market demand, (4) 
faster capacity expansion, (5) favorable FX movement (mainly USD/NTD) for GM and/or 
non-operating items.

Downside risks to our PO are: (1) softer AI server demand due to GPU/components 
shortage, (2) GM pressure due to higher raw material cost (e.g. steel, aluminum), (3) 
softer kitchen slides market demand under macro uncertainty/weakness, (4) delayed 
product certification, (5) slower capacity expansion, (6) unfavorable FX movement 
(mainly USD/NTD) to weigh on GM and/or non-operating items.

King Slide Works Co. Ltd.  | Asia Pacific
Preliminary August Profit and
September Monthly Sales (-31%
MoM/+196% YoY)
Sentiment to stay soft in near term but 2027 outlook is
promising.
King Slide announced its preliminary profit for August, per TWSE regulatory
requirements:
• Revenue was NT$6,466mn (+1% MoM/+344% YoY), 33% of our estimate for
3Q26 of NT$19,555mn (+81% QoQ/+348% YoY) and 39% of consensus' NT
$16,755mn (+55% QoQ/+284% YoY).

• Pretax income was NT$4,826mn (+216% YoY; pretax margin at 74.6%), 29%
of our 3Q26 pretax income estimate of NT$16,377mn (margin at 83.7%) and
34% of consensus' NT$14,094mn (margin at 84.1%).
• Net income was NT$3,819mn (+212% YoY; EPS NT$40.07; NPM 59.1%), 30%
of our 3Q26 net income estimate of NT$12,938mn (EPS NT$135.76; NPM
66.2%) and 34% consensus' NT$11,118mn (EPS NT$114.38; NPM 66.4%).
September monthly sales
• September sales of NT$4,464mn (-31% MoM/+196% YoY), driving 3Q26

revenue to NT$17,338mn (+60% QoQ/+297% YoY), were short of our
estimate of NT$19,555mn (+81% QoQ/+348% YoY) and but ahead of the
consensus forecast of NT$16,755mn (+55% QoQ/+284% YoY).
Our view:
• We think the sequential decline in September  revenue was driven by slower
momentum for some projects, which entered the later part of their product
cycles. While sentiment should stay soft in the near term, we think the
outlook into 2027 is still promising as customers start new product cycles.

• We maintain our view that King Slide's competitiveness in the comprehensive
patent portfolio and strong execution are entrenched and accentuated amid
fast generation migration in AI applications.
• Stay OW with PT of NT$15,000 (30x 2027e P/E).

Valuation Methodology and Risks
King Slide Works Co. Ltd. (2059.TW)
Base case, 30x 2027e P/E. We use price/earnings as our valuation methodology because we
believe it better captures King Slide's earnings growth potential. We believe our target mul-
tiple is justified by an operating profit CAGR of 77% seen for 2025-28, implying a PEG of 0.4
vs. the average of 1.2 over 2010-24, when the stock was trading at 17-18x P/E with an operating

profit CAGR of 14%.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger-than-expected AI capex
n Stronger server demand
n Faster market share gains
Risks to Downside
n Softer AI capex
n Weaker server demand
n Sluggish kitchen furniture demand
n Competition leading to slower share gains.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

### Page 1

ab 8 October 2026
Global Research
Initiation of Coverage
WinWay Technology
Final point of contact: securing AI yield through
advanced testing socket
Solid growth ahead with robust capacity expansions
We initiate coverage of AI/HPC test socket leader WinWay with a Buy rating and NT
$8,900 price target. WinWay's share price has corrected 43% from its April 2026 peak
due to broader market de-rating and margin pressure associated with its Technoprobe

partnership and capacity expansion. We believe the recent correction presents an
opportunity to re-engage with WinWay's long-term growth trajectory, supported by
three key drivers: 1) increasing content from AI GPUs, CPUs, and TPUs, 2) substantial
capacity expansion, and 3) broader adoption of system-level testing (SLT). Together,
these drivers should support revenue growth of 105%/74%/71% in 2026E/27E/28E.
Importantly, WinWay's growth is increasingly being driven by higher socket content per

device rather than semiconductor unit growth alone and the stock currently trades at
16x of our 2028E P/E vs historical average of 30x, potential to re-rate with GM recovery
in the coming quarters. See our companion testing deep dive sector note.
Rising yields and utilisation to offset probe card dilution
Gross margin declined to 38.3% in Q226 from 45.3% in FY25, pressured by outsourced
MEMS probes and expansion-related costs. While probe-card margins are unlikely to

return to H125 levels of 48.9% given the Technoprobe partnership structure, we expect
margin pressure to ease as utilization and yields improve, supporting GM recovery to
43.1% in 2027E and 44.7% in 2028E.
CPU and TPU as fundamental growth drivers
Beyond its core GPU socket franchise,  WinWay is well positioned to benefit from the
expansion of AI-oriented CPU and TPU platforms. We expect the company to supply
sockets for AMD's Venice CPU, Nvidia's Vera CPU, and Google's TPU v8t and v9, and

also Vera CPU probe cards, providing an additional growth engine beyond GPUs.
WinWay is well positioned in this segment due to its historical collaboration with end
customers and its proximity to the local Taiwanese test supply chain. We view AI CPUs
and TPUs as an increasingly important contributor to test interface demand as compute
architectures diversify in the agentic AI era, driving WinWay's socket and probe card
sales growth of 132%/75%/69% and 94%/72%/78% in 2026E/27E/28E, respectively.

We estimate CPU/TPU to contribute  around 30%/20% of WinWay's 2027E sales.
Valuation: Initiate with a Buy rating, with price target of NT$8,900
We forecast EPS of NT$101.8 in 2026E, rising to NT$201.4/393.0 in 2027E/28E. Our NT
$8,900 price target is based on 30x average 2027E-28E P/E, in line with the company's
historical average. We believe this valuation is supported by WinWay's exposure to
structural growth in AI/HPC testing, expanding socket content, and our long-term

earnings CAGR estimate of 30%. We initiate with a Buy rating.

WinWay Technology 8 October 2026 ab 2
WinWay Technology UBS Research
Thesis Map     UBS                    Research     THESIS MAP a guide to our thinking and what´s where in this report
Q: Will capacity constraints limit WinWay's growth in the next two to three years?
Near term, yes. Beyond 2026E, no. WinWay's current capacity remains constrained, but ongoing
expansions should significantly increase output over the next two years. With new capacity coming

online and the company shifting toward higher-value AI/HPC applications, including CPU probe
cards, >10k-pin coaxial sockets and next-generation HyperSockets, we forecast revenue growth of
105%/74%/71% in 2026E/27E/28E, supported by capacity additions, rising AI/HPC content and
expanding socket demand. We also expect operating leverage to improve as capacity scales and
utilization increases, with OPM rising from 27.5% in 2026E to 32.1%/36.7% in 2027E/28E.

Q: Will WinWay's GM recover from Q226's 38.3%?
Yes. We view recent margin pressure as largely temporary. GM has been impacted by MEMS probe
sourcing through Technoprobe and start-up costs associated with capacity expansion. Although
margin dilution from probe card business is unlikely to unravel due to the MEMS partnership terms
with Technoprobe, as new facilities ramp and yields improve, we expect profitability to recover
gradually from the Q226 trough, with gross margin improving to 43.1% in 2027E and 44.7% in

2028E.
We initiate coverage of WinWay with a Buy rating and NT$8,900 price target. We believe the market
is overly focused on near-term margin pressure and underappreciating WinWay's long-term growth
outlook. Key drivers include 1) expanding content from AI GPUs, CPUs, and TPUs, 2) substantial
capacity expansion, and 3) growing adoption of system-level testing (SLT). Together, these drivers
should support revenue growth of 105%/74%/71% in 2026E/27E/28E.

TSMC and OSATs have been expanding their CoWoS capacities aggressively, from 40kwpm in 2024
to around 90 kwpm in 2025, and is tracking to expand CoWoS capacity toward 160kwpm  in 2026E.
TSMC also ramped up expanding N3/N2 capacity, with multiple product launches from customers'
pipelines—Nvidia's Rubin, AMD's MI400 series/Venice CPU and other cloud ASICs—translating into
foreseeable, strong testing demand as the majority are WinWay's customers. AI accelerator test times

have increased meaningfully for new platforms. We expect continued front-end and advanced
packaging investments to drive higher testing intensity and support long-term demand for advanced
sockets and test interfaces.
WinWay's share price has corrected 43% from its April 2026 peak and currently trades at 16x of our
2028E P/E vs historical average of 30x. Our reverse DCF analysis indicates that the current market
price implies only a 19% long-term sales CAGR vs our estimate of 25%, alongside long-term GM

level of 32% vs our estimate of 45%. We see scope for re-rating.

AI/HPC testing, providing final test/system-level test and burn-in test sockets and probe cards through

### Page 3

WinWay Technology 8 October 2026 ab 3
WinWay Technology UBS Research
Testing TAM expanding with IC complexity: leaders likely to
outperform industry growth
Our central view is that testing content per AI accelerator is growing faster than
semiconductor unit volumes, creating a multi-year expansion in testing TAM. We believe
semiconductor testing is entering a period of structural growth as AI-driven chip
complexity increases testing intensity across wafer, package and system levels. The

industry's shift-left strategy, which moves more testing upstream in the semiconductor
manufacturing process, is resulting in longer and more frequent test insertions. As cloud
AI GPUs and ASICs become increasingly complex, additional testing is required to ensure
functionality, performance and yield, while reducing defective parts per million (DPPM).
More robust testing should improve deployment efficiency and lower the cost of
operating AI infrastructure.

Semiconductor testing generally comprises four stages: wafer/chip probing (CP), final
test (FT), system-level test (SLT), and burn-in. CP is conducted at the wafer-level before
packaging, while FT, SLT, and burn-in occur after packaging. The below Figure 1S e m ic o n d u c t o r  t e s t in g  p r o c e s s :  f r o m  w a f e r  le v e l t o  p a c k a g e  le v e l
illustrates the semiconductor manufacturing flow.
Figure 1: Semiconductor testing process: from wafer level to package level

Source: UBS
We estimate semiconductor testing historically accounts for ~1% of total
semiconductor revenue. Within testing, consumables such as probe cards, sockets and
interface boards represent ~10-15% of industry revenue. According to Yole, the probe
card, socket and interface board markets should grow from US$3.8bn, US$2.3bn and
US$1.7bn in 2026 to US$5.5bn, US$3.1bn and US$2.0bn by 2031, respectively.

content and test times rising which each iteration of new AI accelerators/CPU, more
sockets and equipment will be needed in order to increase the throughput.

WinWay is expanding capacity rapidly to capture this growing TAM. Industry test
interface suppliers are expanding capacity aggressively to meet rising AI and HPC
demand. As advanced semiconductors require higher-performance probe cards, sockets
and interface boards, capacity additions are becoming increasingly important to support

growth across both wafer-level and package-level testing.
We believe WinWay is well positioned to gain share within the high-end AI/HPC test
interface market. Taiwanese suppliers benefit from their proximity to leading fabless
customers and the growing preference for localised supply chains, particularly for
advanced testing applications where responsiveness and engineering support are
critical. Against a backdrop of tight industry supply, WinWay announced significant

capacity expansion plans over the next two to three years.
WinWay's capacity is expected by the company to increase from 3.5mn pins per month
at year-end 2025 to 6mn currently, reaching 9mn by year-end 2026 and 14mn by year-
end 2027 through expansions in Renwu, Kaohsiung. By 2027, approximately 60% of
pogo-pin requirements should be internally sourced, strengthening both supply security
and margins.
We estimate these capacity additions will support revenue growth of 105%/74%/71%

in 2026E/27E/28E.

For WinWay, this growth opportunity is substantial, as the capacity expansions for its
socket pins will satisfy around 60% of its own pogo pin  demand in 2027E, we estimate
socket sales will likely contribute for around NT$163 of EPS in 2027E and to further
expand to NT$307 in 2028E. This paves the way for substantial share gaining
opportunity for WinWay for AI/HPC sockets as we believe there are advantages to have

footprint within Taiwan, where there's already an established testing ecosystem with
testing providers such as ASE/SPIL/KYEC, and handler providers such as Hon Precision, all
positioned locally in Taiwan to provide around the clock service and support for AI/HPC
customers.
We see industry capacity tightness to support socket pin ASP growth, as we forecast pin
ASP to grow from US$2.0 in 2026E to US$2.5 in 2027E and US$3.0 in 2028E. We
believe WinWay's capacity expansion creates a significant growth opportunity with a

better margin profile. By 2027E, approximately 60% of pogo pin demand should be
internally sourced, improving both supply security and profitability. We estimate sockets
could contribute around NT$123 of EPS in 2027E, rising to NT$261 in 2028E. Combined
with its expanding capacity, strong customer relationships and proximity to Taiwan's
semiconductor testing ecosystem, including ASE/SPIL, KYEC and major handler
suppliers, WinWay is well positioned to gain share in the AI/HPC socket market.

### Page 6

WinWay Technology 8 October 2026 ab 6
The AI era has materially increased the value of semiconductor testing. Compared with
traditional mobile and automotive chips, AI accelerators incorporate larger compute
dies, more advanced nodes, chiplet architectures, advanced packaging technologies,
multiple I/O dies, and increasing HBM content. These factors drive higher-value test
consumables, including advanced MEMS probe cards, multilayer interface boards, and

next-generation sockets designed to support higher pin counts, thermal loading, and
power delivery requirements.
Capacity expansion by test interface vendors is ultimately supported by continued
investment throughout the advanced packaging ecosystem, from providers such as
TSMC, ASE, Intel, and Amkor. During the Q226 earnings calls, TSMC raised its recent
2026 capex from US$52-56bn to US$60-64bn and ASE raised its 2026 capex from US
$8.5bn to US$10.5bn, with both companies citing robust demand supporting capex

raise, we believe capex for the next two years will be even higher than 2026, indicating
continuous growth in future years. With advanced packaging technologies getting
significantly more complex and with larger form factor, illustrated by the upcoming
ramp of both TSMC's CoPoS (chip-on-panel-on-substrate) and Intel's EMIB-T
(embedded multi-die interconnect bridge) packaging technologies, test interface
solution providers will benefit with more value added consumables (see our CoPoS vs

EMIB-T deep dive).

New iterations of semiconductors, especially for AI / HPC applications, are set to increase
in both front-end wafer fabrication advancements and back-end packaging
complexities with each iterations (i.e. Nvidia's roadmap from Blackwell to Rubin to

Feynman or Google's TPU roadmap from v7 to v8 to v9).   This trend is evident with
TSMC's front-end  and TSMC, Intel, and OSAT's back-end advanced packaging capacity
expansions. For front-end, we see industry N3 capacity expanding from 156kwpm in
year end 2025 to 224/283/293kpwm in year end 2026E/27E/28E, and N2 capacity from
47kwpm in year-end 2025 to 111/200/312kpwm in year end 2026E/27E/28E. For back-
end, we are seeing industry CoWoS capacity to rise from 90kwpm in 2025 to 160kwpm

in 2026E and further expand to 270kwpm in 2027E.

Final-test, system-level test, and burn-in content should continue to increase as AI

accelerators become larger, more power-hungry and more complex. Rising thermal
design power and packaging complexity increase validation requirements, driving

### Page 7

WinWay Technology 8 October 2026 ab 7
longer test times and higher demand for test equipment to prevent testing from
becoming a production bottleneck. This is where WinWay's sockets comes into play—
as high-end chips with 2.5D/3D/3.5D packaging requires intricate FT sockets to handle
the high thermal design power (TDP) and high data rates of up to 224G during the FT
process for reliable test results.

While architectural changes can affect test requirements for individual product
generations, we expect the long-term trend of rising testing content to remain intact.
For example, Nvidia's Rubin Ultra adopts a simpler package structure than previously
expected, from a 4-die structure to a 2-die structure and Rubin Ultra HBM de-spec from
HBM4E 12-Hi to initially use HBM4 8-Hi and upgrade to HBM4E 8-Hi at the latter stages,

which may moderate near-term test growth. However, future products such as Nvidia's
Feynman are expected to reintroduce greater package complexity, with a four-GPU per
package structure. More importantly, any reduction in test time can be offset by higher
production volumes, making throughput requirements equally important for testing
demand.
We expect test interface content growth to outpace unit growth, For instance, test
socket's ASP directly correlates with its pin counts, with the addition of 20-30% more

pins inside the sockets, socket ASP can grow 20-30% or more as the socket might
require significant cooling capabilities as the TDP of the DUT rises with its larger form
factor. Unlike prior semiconductor cycles, much of the current demand is driven by rising
test interface content per device rather than unit growth alone.
Robust growth drivers across semiconductor testing
We believe WinWay is set to benefit from several semiconductor testing growth drivers

and capture the AI induced, expanding testing TAM. We see three main growth drivers
for WinWay: 1) AI accelerators (GPU + TPU), 2) agentic AI driven CPU demand, and 3)
shift-left testing trend. Below we list the three main growth drivers in detail.
Accelerators still in focus with TPU as another significant growth driver
Nvidia has been the primary driver of testing content growth over the past several years,
benefiting the entire semiconductor testing supply chain. We expect this trend to

continue as GPU architectures evolve from Blackwell to Rubin and then to Feynman,
with increasing process node complexity, larger package sizes and more advanced
packaging technologies driving higher testing value per device.
We believe Nvidia should remain a major contributor to  WinWay, with GPUs continuing
to serve as their core growth engine. We estimate WinWay's socket share could improve
from a roughly 50/50 split with Smiths Interconnect in prior generations to 60/40 with

Rubin. Nvidia currently accounts for approximately 50% of WinWay's revenue. Both
Nvidia GPUs and Google TPUs increase testing demand through higher content per
device rather than unit growth alone.

### Page 8

WinWay Technology 8 October 2026 ab 8
Beyond Nvidia, Google's TPU roadmap represents a significant incremental growth
opportunity.  In our view, WinWay should benefit as the socket supplier for both TPU v8t
and the next-generation TPU v9 platform. We believe MediaTek's increasing share gain
for Google TPUs will also benefit the local Taiwanese test interface supply chain. We
estimate TPU to contribute to around 20% of WinWay 2027E sales.
We view TPU v9 as a particularly attractive opportunity given its large four-die package

architecture based on Intel's EMIB-T packaging technology, which should drive higher
testing content. We estimate production of 4mn TPU v8t units in 2027E, followed by
2.3mn TPU v9 packages (9.2mn dies) and an additional 2mn TPU v8t units in 2028E.
Importantly, while EMIB-T packaging is expected to be performed in Malaysia, we
believe the majority of advanced testing value should remain within the Taiwanese
testing ecosystem. Leading testing providers, including ASE and KYEC, maintain

substantial testing operations across Taiwan and Southeast Asia, supporting continued
participation by Taiwanese test interface suppliers. See our latest MediaTek report and
EMIB-T deep dive.
CPU business as a supplemental catalyst for test interface companies in
the agentic world
We believe semiconductor test interface are an underappreciated beneficiary of the
emerging agentic AI ecosystem. UBS estimates the CPU market will expand to US
$170bn by 2030E from US$31bn in 2025 and US$71bn in 2027E, driven by rising

deployment of AI infrastructure. As CPU-to-GPU ratios increasingly approach 1:1 in AI
clusters, demand for testing interfaces should expand alongside CPU volumes. TSMC is
the main CP test provider in the industry, with probe card suppliers aiding its massive
demand by supplying probes, such as TSMC's GPU probe cards supported by
Technoprobe's probes. However, with capacity now becoming scarce with strong front
and back-end demand, TSMC is outsourcing its CP business to ASE and KYEC, further

broadening the testing supply chain. This is where new probe card vendors come in—
smaller probe card players in the past, such as  WinWay, now has the opportunity to gain
significant, non-GPU share from TSMC, as they have MEMS probe capabilities
(WinWay's MEMS probe are from its strategic partnership with Technoprobe) and has a
long established relationship with the fabless companies such as Nvidia via supplying
non-probe card consumables such as sockets. Of which, CPU projects has become the

main area for  WinWay to expand their footprint.
We expect WinWay to benefit from the ramp of Nvidia's ARM-based Vera CPU and
Google's ARM-based Axion CPU in 2026E-27E. WinWay is positioned to supply sockets
and probe cards for Vera and sockets for Axion. Additional upside could come from
AMD's Venice CPU, where WinWay is well positioned. For WinWay, CPU-related
demand could account for approximately 30% of sales in 2026E-27E. Importantly, CPU
programmes diversify exposure beyond AI GPUs and provide a second structural growth

driver for test interface suppliers.
Shift-left igniting secular growth for chip probing
The industry's adoption of shift-left testing should drive a multi-year structural growth
opportunity for chip probing (CP) and its supply chain. As advanced packaging costs rise
and AI accelerators become increasingly complex, semiconductor manufacturers are
placing greater emphasis on identifying known-good dies (KGD) before packaging. The
test shift-left trend will  drive structural AI/HPC probe card and test consumables

demand, with increasing test interface content as test insertions and test time increase.
WinWay should also benefit through its partnership with Technoprobe, with probe card
revenue growth remaining robust at 94%/72% in 2026E/27E following 161%/260%
growth in 2024/25.

The economics of advanced packaging reinforce this trend. Heterogeneous integration,
chiplet architectures and 2.5D/3D packaging significantly increase package value, while

defects identified late in the manufacturing process can lead to substantial scrap costs.
To mitigate this risk, foundries and OSATs such as TSMC and ASE employ distributed
testing flows incorporating KGD, known-good-stack (KGS) and assembly test screening,
to catch defects before the components enter a multi-week packaging cycle (>2 weeks
for CoW and 2 weeks for oS). The importance of these tests rises as AI clusters become
increasingly dependent on component reliability and low defective-parts-per-million

(DPPM) rates. Given the multiplicative yield degradation, a 300bps yield decline at just
one manufacturing stage (e.g. dropping from 99.5% to 96.5%) reduces overall
integrated four-stage yield from 98.0% to 95.0%; on a 500,000 unit ramp at US
$10,000 per unit, this 3% scrap delta translates into around US$150m in unrecoverable
losses given >6-month manufacturing lead times.
At the same time, rising chip power consumption is increasing the technical difficulty of

wafer-level testing. As chip thermal design power scales beyond 1,000W and power
density is expected by the industry to surge  another 8x by 2030, traditional wafer-level
chip probe (WLCP) encounters severe physical bottlenecks. Finer probe pitches and
higher power densities increase the risk of overheating, probe needle burnout and yield
degradation, creating demand for more advanced probing and thermal-control
solutions.

WinWay  was founded in 2001 in Kaohsiung, Taiwan, and is a leading provider of
semiconductor testing interface solutions, with test sockets representing its core
business. The company focuses on advanced AI/HPC applications, supplying sockets for

final test (FT), system-level test (SLT) and burn-in, as well as cobra probe cards  and
partnership with Technoprobe for MEMS probe cards.
WinWay's business is primarily divided into two segments:
 Test socket: Sockets are WinWay's largest revenue contributor. The company
supplies a broad range of testing sockets, including coaxial, PoP/plastic and burn-
in sockets, serving applications ranging from consumer electronics to high-
performance computing.

 Coaxial socket: utilised as the mainstream option for nearly all AI/HPC chips,
as traditional sockets are near its physical limits. Coaxial sockets need to handle
data rates of up to 224G for high speed transmission and high chip TDP during
the FT process.
 HyperSocket: a rubber-silicone like layer called elastomer are placed on top
of the pogo pins, these elastomer pins causes zero damage to the chips being
tested under high contact forces. WinWay's next generation HyperSocket

technology utilises elastomers to provide a softer contact between the DUT
and the pogo pins, avoiding damage to solder ball. This structure significantly
enhances spring probe's lifetime, reduces cleaning frequency, and achieves
maximum production efficiency. We estimate it will contribute around 5% of
sales in 2027E with a 20-30% ASP premium as compared to standard coaxial
socket.
 Probe cards: WinWay historically supplies cobra probe cards (vertical probe

cards), but with the company announcing their five-year probe supply deal with
Technoprobe for MEMS probe in December 2025, they are now one of Nvida Vera
CPU's main probe card suppliers.
AI accelerators are pushing socket requirements beyond 10,000 pins, making coaxial
sockets the industry standard for leading-edge AI/HPC devices. These sockets must
support transmission speeds of up to 224G while handling significantly higher thermal
design power (TDP) during testing.

Elastomer technology is increasingly being adopted alongside spring-probe (pogo-pin)
architectures to reduce chip damage and improve contact reliability. WinWay's next-
generation HyperSocket integrates elastomer materials between the DUT and spring
probes, improving probe lifetime, reducing cleaning requirements and enhancing
production efficiency. We believe HyperSocket could become an important
differentiator as next-generation AI accelerators demand higher pin counts, bandwidth

and thermal performance. We estimate HyperSocket could contribute approximately
5% of WinWay's revenue in 2027E while carrying a 20-30% ASP premium versus
conventional coaxial sockets. The transition toward larger AI packages, higher pin
counts and faster data-transfer speeds should continue to increase socket content per
device. We expect this trend to support long-term growth in the socket market, with
technology leaders such as WinWay well positioned to gain share through next-

generation solutions including HyperSocket.
Beyond its existing product portfolio, WinWay is developing next-generation
technologies including CPO electrical test sockets and liquid-cooled sockets for future
high TDP AI accelerators, reinforcing its position within the AI/HPC testing ecosystem.
